### Post #6: 2025-04-07 20:54 — `$HUMA` `[Dd]`

**$HUMA - Market is sleeping on a miracle**

Humacyte ($HUMA) has recently been wrecked but the setup is still stupid good. Last month they conducted a public offering, selling 25 million shares at $2.00 each, raising approximately $50 million in gross proceeds. Between this and tariff fear the stock has dropped 60+%, but the dilution gave them the cash they need to roll out Symvess, fund the pipeline, and actually start turning this into a real business. 

They’ve already got FDA approval for Symvess, the first bioengineered human vessel. It’s shipping now to trauma centers. This thing was used in Ukraine on battlefield injuries. Real patients. Real limbs saved. In simple terms, it’s a lab-grown blood vessel made from human cells, but without any living cells left in it, so the body doesn’t reject it. Surgeons can implant it off the shelf, and over time it actually becomes part of the patient’s body. This isn’t R&D. This is already happening.

And it’s the same tech being used across multiple massive markets. One platform. Multiple shots on goal.

Trauma is already approved. This covers extremity vascular injuries where a vein graft isn’t an option. Market’s around 1 to 2 billion annually.

Dialysis access is in Phase 3 now. They’ll file with the FDA in 2026. This is a multi-billion dollar market on its own. Around 3.5 million people globally are on dialysis. A huge number of them need better vascular access solutions. Fresenius is involved here which says a lot.

Peripheral Arterial Disease is next. Late-stage pipeline. More than 100,000 bypass surgeries in the US alone every year. Total market is over 6 billion globally.

Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting is in preclinical now. Smaller diameter version of the vessel. 500,000 CABG procedures a year in the US. TAM is over 10 billion.

They’re also exploring future stuff like a BioVascular Pancreas for Type 1 diabetes and bioengineered organs like lungs and trachea. Combined with everything else, the company puts total TAM at over 150 billion.

Cash position is strong after the recent raise. About 140 million in the bank. Market cap is around the same. You’re basically getting the entire pipeline for free. Commercial revenue just started. They’re guiding to profitability around 2027. Tariffs aren’t a real factor. Manufacturing is US-based.

This isn’t a science experiment. It’s a first-to-market platform with real world use, FDA approval, and a deep pipeline built on the same foundational tech. The market is pricing it like it’s going to zero.

I’m in for 50K shares. I think this goes way higher once people stop reacting to the dilution and look at what they’re actually building.

### Post #8: 2025-04-16 15:02 — `$MP` `[Dd]`

**MP Materials ($MP): The One U.S. Company That Actually Solves the Rare Earth Problem**

Rare earth minerals are the backbone of modern technology. They go into EV motors, wind turbines, missiles, fighter jets, smartphones…pretty much anything with a motor, speaker, or sensor. The most valuable ones are Neodymium and Praseodymium, used to make permanent magnets that are essential for both electrification and defense.

China controls over 90 percent of global rare earth processing and almost all of the world’s magnet manufacturing. Even when the raw materials come from elsewhere, they almost always get sent to China to be refined. That is a massive geopolitical risk.

MP Materials is the only company in the U.S. mining rare earths at scale. Their Mountain Pass mine in California is one of the richest deposits in the world. It produces over 45,000 metric tons of rare earth oxide content annually, with a heavy focus on light rare earths like Neodymium and Praseodymium.

MP is building the full supply chain. Phase I is mining. Phase II, which is now operational, is refining NdPr in-house instead of sending it to China. Phase III is magnet manufacturing, and that’s already underway in Texas. They are producing metal now and expect to deliver commercial neodymium-iron-boron magnets by the end of this year. No other U.S. company is close to this level of vertical integration.

Lynas and Energy Fuels are other key non-Chinese players. Lynas mines in Australia and refines in Malaysia, but they are not making magnets. Energy Fuels is working on monazite sand processing, but they are tiny and still early. MP is the only real U.S. player with scale, infrastructure, and end-to-end capability.

They have deals locked in with GM and the Department of Defense. They received a 58 million dollar tax credit for their magnet facility and 100 million in customer prepayments. And now, the White House just signed an executive order opening a Section 232 investigation into critical mineral imports. That only makes MP more important.

Short-term earnings are down due to lower rare earth prices and heavy investment, but long-term this is a strategic asset. Despite the recent pop, the market still isn’t pricing in what it means to be the only domestic mine-to-magnet solution in the middle of a global supply chain reset.

I bought 1,000 shares yesterday.


*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/LgZ/mp-materials-dollarmp-the-one-*

### Post #9: 2025-04-16 17:21 — `$NVDA` `[Discuss]`

**Nvidia’s $5.5B Write-Down Isn’t What It Seems**

A lot of people are taking Nvidia’s $5.5B inventory write-down as a sign they’re screwed on China sales. That the A800 and H800 chips they built specifically to comply with earlier export rules are now worthless bricks. But let’s be clear. This isn’t about them giving up on finding buyers. It’s accounting.

GAAP rules force companies to value inventory at the lower of cost or market. If a product loses its primary customer base, like what just happened with China due to tightening US export controls, accountants don’t care if you might still sell it later. They make you write it down now. Even if there’s a resale path. Even if there’s value left on the table.

This is Nvidia doing the smart thing. Take the hit, reset expectations, avoid giving regulators or auditors any reason to circle back. It’s conservative, maybe overly so, but it’s not a signal of panic. It’s a signal of control.

The $5.5B isn’t dead weight. Nvidia just isn’t allowed to say otherwise on their balance sheet. Good time to go long on $NVDA given this overreaction. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/Lh2/nvidias-dollar55b-write-down-i*

### Post #10: 2025-04-16 21:40 `[Discuss]`

**The real intent behind the tariffs is becoming harder to ignore**

This was never about protecting American workers or bringing jobs home. It’s about isolating China.

The pattern is clear. Countries were targeted not because they threaten the U.S. directly, but because they support China’s position in the global economy. Some are end-markets for Chinese goods. Others serve as intermediaries, moving Chinese products into U.S. supply chains. Many are embedded in the infrastructure that enables China to scale and export.

Equally important is who wasn’t hit with reciprocal tariffs. India, Brazil, and Indonesia received base-level tariffs with no retaliation. These are not oversights. They are strategic pivots. The U.S. wants them in play, not pushed deeper into Beijing’s orbit.

Japan and Australia were included narrowly, avoiding their most critical exports. Canada was handled inconsistently. Mexico, on the other hand, was directly and deliberately targeted. It serves as a key channel for Chinese components entering the U.S. under the cover of nearshoring. The message was clear. That route is being shut down.

The formula used to determine tariff levels was opaque by design. It was a deliberately complex (and bullshit) equation tied to production overcapacity, giving cover to selectively apply pressure.

U.S. officials have now said it outright. Dependence on China is a negotiating tool. This is not a trade war. It is economic containment. The goal is to fracture the network around Beijing, starve its export system, and force global realignment.

Even the inflammatory rhetoric fits. This isn’t sloppy messaging. It’s provocation. The U.S. needs a clear enemy. Something to unify around. Something the public and its allies can align against. China is being cast into that role.

This won’t be clean. Some moves will look symbolic. Others will seem misdirected or chaotic. But when you step back, the pattern holds. The strategy is coordinated. The escalation is intentional. And the effects will compound.

So how does this play out in the market?

If the administration is successful with its efforts, supply chains will be forcibly rewired. Companies and countries will be pushed to find alternatives to Chinese production, which will drive up costs and shift capital. That is structurally inflationary and favors nations aligned with U.S. interests.

Expect capital to flow into emerging markets like Mexico, India, and parts of Southeast Asia. Domestic manufacturing will see tailwinds. Commodities tied to energy, infrastructure, and critical minerals will catch a bid. The West will prioritize control over efficiency.

At the same time, China will respond with its own bloc-building. That accelerates decoupling and raises demand for neutral assets. Gold, Bitcoin, and scarce hard assets will benefit as global trust fractures.

This is not a temporary policy move. It’s a long-term reshaping of global trade and capital alignment.

You don’t trade the tariffs. You position for the rewiring.

How are you playing this?

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/Lkb/the-real-intent-behind-the-tar*

### Post #15: 2025-04-18 16:57 `[Discuss]`

**Some career advice**

In my prior life, I played the role of an executive in Corporate America. For any aspiring young leaders out there, make sure you negotiate a change in control agreement when you make it to the room where it happens. It can be life-changing. 

I thought I would share a story from early in my career that definitely has no applicability to anything else going on in the world currently but may help you in your career. 

I used to work under a man who thought he was the sun, and the rest of us were just planets lucky enough to orbit him. He’d enter a room like it was a stage, spew half-coherent nonsense with absolute conviction, then demand applause for having solved problems he couldn’t define or for solving problems that he himself created. 

You couldn’t manage him, you had to manipulate him. I’d take the real plan, sprinkle it with his favorite buzzwords, tell him it was inspired by something he once said (even if he hadn’t), and let him “discover” it like some cosmic genius. Within hours, he’d be parading it around like it was the greatest idea in human history, conveniently forgetting it was the exact thing he shut down a week before. We let him believe every win was his. That was the only way anything got done.

We weren’t staff. We were handlers. Babysitters for an ego with executive power. We built walls around his impulses, slipped real work in through side doors, and leaked praise like breadcrumbs to keep him distracted while we tried to prevent disaster.

The scariest part? He genuinely believed he was doing it all himself. That the applause was real. That we were lucky to have him. It was never leadership. It was a reality show.

But hey, mission accomplished, right?

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/Lsk/some-career-advice*

### Post #17: 2025-04-18 20:19 `[Discuss]`

**PSA: If They’re Losing in This Chop, Why Are You Following Their Moves**

This isn’t just a rough patch. It’s a filter. Anyone can win in a bull market. Up-only hides a lot of fraud, luck, and blind confidence. 

But this chop? This is the real test.

It separates traders from characters. Killers from cosplayers. And a lot of what I’m seeing right now? Cosplay. 

Ask yourself:

Do they ever show their full portfolio and results or just the occasional W?
Are they down bad and blaming the market/manipulation/bad luck or anything else other than taking accountability?
Are they pushing the same tired setups while conditions scream for adaptation?
Are half their posts telling you how awesome they are instead of proving it?

There’s a time for fundamentals
A time for technicals
A time for macro, sentiment, flow, and even gut instinct for the truly gifted 
But real traders shift. Losers cling.

And while they’re making excuses, you’re missing the opportunity of a lifetime. This chop is where fortunes are made if you’re paying attention.

If someone isn’t adapting, isn’t transparent, and isn’t eating. You’re not learning from them. You’re feeding them. 

Wake up before you miss your chance. 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/LsQ/psa-if-theyre-losing-in-this-c*

### Post #22: 2025-04-22 04:45 — `$MP` `[Dd]`

**$MP or Bust: Even non-investors better hope they succeed**

Listed below are the top 50 assets owned by AfterHour users with an indication of how much each is exposed to a growing national security and supply chain crisis which many of you don’t fully appreciate. 

Until recently, the U.S. depended on China to process over 90% of the world’s rare earth elements (REEs). But with China halting REE exports and tariffs cutting off processing access, that pipeline is closed.

Now the entire tech, EV, defense, and AI stack is left hoping one company can step up: MP Materials ($MP) - the only scaled U.S. rare earth miner.

$MP still isn’t fully ready. Although Phase 1 (mining capacity) is likely scalable, its full value to U.S. industry depends on two buildouts. Phase 2: Domestic REE processing which is underway, targeted for full capacity in late 2025. Phase 3: U.S. magnet manufacturing targeted for full capacity in late 2026–early 2027. 

A recent executive order could unlock fast-tracked government funding to accelerate build-out and there are a few partnerships that could get us through the hump leveraging capacity in Australia, Japan, Korea and a handful of other countries, but this is shaping up to be a critical issue for many industries. Considering this has defense applicability, you better believe DOD is going to get what they need first, despite the clown currently running that shit show. 

So whether you choose to invest in $MP or not, we all need to be rooting them on unless you are riding the hopium that either Trump or Xi is going to bow down to the other and agree to freely cooperate on the most critical resources on Earth. Except maybe the BTC and MSTR purists out there, you guys are golden. 

Top 50 Assets & Their Rare Earth Exposure

	1.	NVDA (NVIDIA) – High: Chips, data centers, AI accelerators require REE-based cooling and semis

### Post #26: 2025-04-23 18:18 — `$LTBR, $UUUU, $MP` `[Dd]`

**The Reactor We Abandoned Is Running in China**

In the 1950s the US had a choice. Thorium or uranium. One was safer, more efficient, and nearly impossible to weaponize. The other made bombs. We stayed on brand. We chose bombs.

Thorium got shelved. Not because it failed. Because it didn’t fit the military playbook.

Now China is running a molten salt reactor using thorium, using our own research. It doesn’t melt down. It doesn’t need high-pressure cooling. It leaves almost no long-lived radioactive waste. And it runs on a fuel the US already has sitting in the ground and in waste piles.

This matters. It opens a path to energy independence that doesn’t rely on Russian enrichment or traditional uranium. It’s a different way to play nuclear and a different way to play the geopolitical shift.

I’ve been positioned for rare earths. That continues. But if the July 14 Department of Energy interim report on domestic critical mineral strategy mentions thorium even once it could trigger something bigger. It would mean we are finally reconsidering what we left behind.

$UUUU is the direct thorium mining play. They are already stockpiling monazite, a rare earth mineral that contains significant concentrations of thorium. What was once treated as radioactive waste is now potential reactor fuel. I picked up 300 July 18 $5 calls. High risk, high reward play. 

$MP is still my anchor. The thorium concentration is lower but it is there. Right now it is waste. That could change fast and provide another revenue stream for $MP. 

$LTBR is the tech. I grabbed 3500 shares. If the US ever moves toward molten salt systems they are set up to be a part of that future.

This is not a bet on a headline. It is a position built around what comes next if the US stops playing catch-up and starts leapfrogging.

If thorium shows up in that report I want to be holding. If it doesn’t I still like the setup.

### Post #27: 2025-04-23 22:58 — `$MP` `[Discuss]`

**Too Bad Chinese History was an Elective**

At the time, History of Rock sounded a lot cooler. Unfortunately, the cultural impact of the Beatles in the 1960s ain’t doing much for my portfolio right now.

So let’s briefly explore Chinese history. Because if you think China will blink first, you don’t understand who we’re dealing with. 

The Century of Humiliation (1839 to 1949)
A hundred years of invasion, occupation, and carved-up sovereignty. Western powers flooded China with opium, seized ports, imposed unequal treaties, and dictated trade.
Then came Japan with brutality, atrocities, and occupation.
China didn’t forget. It didn’t move on. It built a national identity around one vow: never again.

The Great Leap Forward (1958 to 1962)
Mao tried to industrialize the nation overnight. Peasants built backyard furnaces to meet steel quotas, melting tools and pots while crops rotted in the fields. At least 45 million died. There was no rebellion. Only silence, starvation, and more state control.

The Cultural Revolution (1966 to 1976)
A decade of purges, book burnings, public beatings, and institutional collapse. When it ended, the Party emerged stronger, more centralized, and untouchable.

Tiananmen Square (1989)
Students demanded freedom. The regime sent tanks. The world was horrified. Beijing didn’t blink.

Hong Kong (1997 to 2020)
They waited nearly a century to take it back, then stripped its autonomy in plain view while the world watched and moved on.

Tech Crackdowns (2020 to 2022)
China erased over one trillion dollars in market cap from its own companies to remind billionaires and investors who’s in charge. Control matters more than growth. Always.

China is playing the long game. They’re not chasing quarterly wins. They’re playing for global hegemony. They’re willing to wait, to suffer, while we panic.

So how does this play out between a country that embraces pain as a virtue and a narcissist who’s stuck his neck out and now needs to save face?

We focus on the components we absolutely cannot afford to lose, the ones that would bring critical systems to a halt if they stop moving. We drag out negotiations. We secure just enough relief to avoid collapse. And we buy time to (try to) rebuild what we should have built years ago.

The monopoly China holds on rare earth processing, the materials behind EVs, missiles, satellites, phones, power grids, and defense, is the most critical piece on the board. And it puts us at a severe negotiating disadvantage.

$MP is the long-term path to independence. But in the near term, there’s no realistic way to avoid relying on China to keep our systems running. 

We have three, maybe six months, before our supply chain dependence becomes critical. Without relief, EV production halts, power grid expansion stalls, missile systems slip, medical imaging breaks down, and chip supply chains begin to fracture. 

And China knows it.

Given how aggressively we’ve positioned ourselves, this won’t be a clean tit for tat negotiation. If we want continued access to China’s rare earth processing, tariff reductions alone won’t cut it.

We’re going to have to give up more than we admit. We’ll quietly make concessions to get critical materials flowing again. And we’ll dress it up in other terms: strategic, temporary, necessary.

As talks begin, don’t watch the headlines. Watch what we give up to keep things running.

This isn’t a reset. This is survival. And China holds the real Trump card, rare earth processing.

### Post #35: 2025-04-28 20:01 — `$TSLA` `[Discuss]`

**I Trade Narratives, Not Numbers**

Narrative investing just clicks for me. At its core, it’s simple: narrative is the story people are telling themselves about what’s happening and what’s about to happen.

And right now, stories are running the market.

Humans are wired for this. Since the beginning of civilization, stories have shaped how we see the world. They make complex stuff simple. They tap emotions way faster than logic ever could. MRI scans even show our brains literally light up differently when we hear a story. It’s built into us.

I don’t believe we’re rational beings. I believe we’re rationalizing beings. We need a story to make sense of the world, whether it’s true or not. The same thing happens in markets. A little bit of new information shows up. Someone builds a story around it. If it hits the right emotional nerve, it spreads like wildfire.

Right now, maybe it’s hope for a China trade deal. Maybe it’s fear of tariffs wrecking supply chains. Maybe it’s belief that the Fed has inflation under control. Maybe you’re clinging to the idea that your beloved stock that will change the world just can’t go any lower.

Whatever it is, it’s the emotion inside the story that gets people moving, not the raw data. And once a narrative takes hold, confirmation bias kicks in. People grab onto anything that supports the story and ignore anything that doesn’t. It feels like the story is getting stronger, but really people are just getting deeper into the belief loop. 

Just look at $TSLA if you want a real-world case study.

That’s how you get big moves disconnected from fundamentals. That’s why narratives are so hard to break. Usually it takes a totally new story, or overwhelming evidence, before people even start to question it.

So ask yourself:
What’s your narrative for each of your investments? Does it line up with the macro narrative playing out right now? Is your narrative real or just hope dressed up to sound smart?

Because you’re not trading numbers right now. You’re trading belief. And strong beliefs will wreck your technicals and fundamentals. Belief is a lot stronger, and a lot more dangerous, than most people realize. 

Most people invest in what they can see. I invest in what they believe. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/MdW/i-trade-narratives-not-numbers*

### Post #38: 2025-05-01 16:48 — `$RTX, $VWAGY, $MP` `[News]`

**Pentagon Transfers AI Metals Tool to Private Sector**

The Pentagon has transferred control of its AI metals pricing system to a private nonprofit backed by major industrial players, including Volkswagen ($VWAGY), MP Materials ($MP), and RTX Corporation ($RTX). The group spans sectors from automotive and rare earth mining to defense, highlighting the strategic importance of building a secure and transparent critical minerals supply chain. 

The tool models fair market pricing for critical minerals based on real production costs, giving Western buyers more confidence to strike long-term deals without relying on China’s opaque pricing and market manipulation. 

Valuations of critical mineral companies are closely tied to the price of the underlying materials. China has long exploited this by flooding the market to suppress prices, undermining Western producers and maintaining dominance. The AI tool counters that by offering transparent, cost-based benchmarks. If adopted widely, it could stabilize pricing, support Western projects, and reshape valuation dynamics across the sector.

DARPA will continue funding it, but private industry is now in control. It’s a serious move toward redefining the critical minerals supply chain and reducing exposure to China’s pricing power.

The July 14 interim report from the Section 232 investigation should offer more detail on the broader plan to reduce dependence on China, but the U.S. clearly isn’t waiting to take necessary actions to redefine the global supply chain.

### Post #39: 2025-05-02 12:45 `[Discuss]`

**You Didn’t Get Rugged, You Got Reminded**

Some of y’all threw money into a coin that launched like a toddler with a jetpack and now you’re shocked it didn’t land clean.

I said it earlier this week in my trading narratives post. We’re not rational beings, we’re rationalizing beings. And we’re damn good at crafting stories to protect our egos. When you made the play, it was about “supporting the community.” But when the price tanked, suddenly it was a scam. A rug pull.

We’ve all taken swings that didn’t land. But if you actually want to get better, you’ve got to own the decision, not just the result. If the coin had mooned, it would’ve still been the same bet, with the same risk, based on the same thin info. The only difference is we’d be seeing YOLO posts about your gains instead of blame.

So let’s call it what it was. Because it certainly wasn’t a calculated decision with all the due diligence you normally bring to your decisions. It was a reflex. A flash of hype, not a thought-out move. And when it falls apart, the first place to look isn’t the dev, it’s the person in the mirror.

Growth starts when the excuses stop. But for those not quite ready to take accountability, no worries. Drop some fresh excuses in the comments. Just make sure none of them are your fault.

### Post #46: 2025-05-16 23:59 — `$MSTY, $MTPLF, $IBIT, $MSTR` `[Dd]`

**Downgraded. Another Spark in a Slow Burn.**

“The budget should be balanced, the treasury refilled… lest Rome fall.” - Cicero

Rome didn’t fall overnight. It eroded slowly through debt, division, and distraction. The people didn’t feel collapse. They felt rising prices, empty promises, and a system that quietly stopped working. Their money bought less. Their leaders talked more. And nothing really changed.

Rome didn’t listen until it was too late. And we’re walking the same path, just with better branding and high-speed internet.

Moody’s downgrade isn’t an isolated event. It’s the third in a pattern. And if history is any guide, the aftermath won’t be loud at first, it will be subtle, disorienting, and easy to dismiss.

But the signs were there the last two times too. Let’s look at what happened in the immediate aftermath of the last two downgrades. Not to be alarmist. But to be honest about where this story tends to go.

2011:
S&P strips the U.S. of its AAA credit rating for the first time ever. Markets panic. S&P 500 drops 17% in two weeks. Gold rips. Treasury yields fall (flight to “safety,” ironically). Bitcoin? $10 at the time. Irrelevant. 

2023:
Fitch joins the chorus. Same reasons: political dysfunction, soaring debt, no adult supervision. Markets shrug temporarily. Bitcoin jumps 30% in the weeks that follow. Not noise anymore. Smart money starts stepping outside the system. 

Now, 2025:
Moody’s finally folds. The last AAA rating is gone. For the first time in modern history, no major agency considers U.S. debt risk-free.

This isn’t just about ratings. This is about faith. Faith in fiscal policy. Faith in fiat. Faith in a system built on IOUs, hope, and hubris. 

So what happens next? Short-term chop? Probably. Another “flight to safety”? Maybe. But if the unwind accelerates and trust really breaks, money won’t flee to the dollar. It’ll flee the whole game.

In 2011, Bitcoin wasn’t invited to the table. By 2023, it was standing at the threshold, unproven, but impossible to ignore as cracks kept forming. Now it’s not just at the table. It’s eyeing the head seat, ready to flip it if the system buckles. 

Bitcoin isn’t the answer to everything and it doesn’t have to be. But when faith in fiat and the U.S. begins to fracture, it becomes the step that makes the most sense. It’s not the only answer, but it’s the one that fits this moment. And like those who saw Rome’s decline early, it’s about knowing when to stop walking the old road and where the next one might begin.

$MSTR $MSTY $IBIT $MTPLF

**Are you listening Part II?**

Buried in the One Big Beautiful Bill Act (yes, that’s the real name), there’s a quiet $2.5B allocation “to improve the United States production of critical minerals through the National Defense Stockpile…”

Translation: the U.S. is preparing to stockpile rare earths.

Now pair that with $MP CEO James Litinsky, who just said it would take “single-digit billions” to fully onshore the rare earth supply chain. This bill may very well be a big step in that direction. The full interview (linked below) is worth your time. James lays it all out clearly.

### Post #53: 2025-05-25 23:17 `[Discuss]`

**The 4-year Bitcoin Cycle is Dead**

It made sense when retail drove the market and halving headlines created momentum. But Bitcoin has outgrown that phase. Today, it trades as a macro asset shaped by liquidity flows, institutional demand, fiat instability, and the occasional Trump tweet, not post-halving surges. 

The halving used to matter because supply shocks impacted price in a shallow, retail-led market. Now, ETFs alone are absorbing more daily supply than miners produce. The marginal buyer is no longer a retail trader chasing narratives. It’s institutions, asset managers, and increasingly, corporate treasuries allocating for long-term exposure.

Global liquidity, not block rewards, is what moves markets now.

Central banks are backed into a corner. Inflation is sticky, debt loads are unsustainable, and real yields are negative across much of the developed world. The only path forward is slow-motion debasement. The U.S. fiscal position is eroding, deficits are ballooning, and the dollar is being wielded more aggressively as a geopolitical tool, driving capital to seek neutral stores of value outside the traditional system.

Bitcoin is benefiting directly from this shift.

Foreign buyers, wealth preservation offices, and corporates are treating BTC less like a trade and more like strategic insurance. This is a structural change.

The idea that price action will follow the same script as previous cycles is increasingly disconnected from how the asset actually functions today. The demand profile has changed. The market structure has changed. Bitcoin’s role in the global system has changed. Investors who still rely on the old model are misreading what this market is becoming.

### Post #58: 2025-06-01 20:51 — `$MP` `[Discuss]`

**My Best Performing Investment This Year: Stress Management**

Better decisions come from a clearer head, and I’ve found that managing stress is just as important as managing risk. I’ve been refining my stress and sleep routine lately and it’s had a real impact on clarity, discipline, and how I handle volatility (in markets and life). Here’s what I aspire to do every week. 

Run 2-3 miles (3x/week): Low-intensity cardio. Regulates cortisol, boosts dopamine, and keeps mental fog in check.

Cold exposure: Showers or baths. Usually showers. I’ll end my normal shower with 2 minutes of cold. Sharpens focus and helps reset nervous system. Builds discipline. I want to get a cold plunge but haven’t pulled the trigger yet. 

Wim Hof breathing: Get a bit of a natural high after doing this sometimes. Sounds weird, but it really helps clear mental noise and reset when stress builds. It’s also a bit like meditation given how focused you are on your breathing.

Meditation: 10–20 min/day. Recently got marketed Lumenate App on TikTok. It’s trippy. Surprisingly effective for deep calm. Only been a week but I think I’ll stick with it. Damn TikTok algo is dialed in to taking my money. 

Sauna: I have a sauna blanket. Prefer a regular sauna, but this gets the job done. I usually do this in the evening. Lowers cortisol, relieves tension, and noticeably improves sleep quality.

I also take L-theanine which helps with stress and Magnesium Glycinate for sleep.

For me, good sleep is the unlock. I wouldn’t say I’ve ever really struggled with sleep, but putting effort towards quality sleep has made a massive improvement for me. Everything else compounds better when that’s dialed in.

Anything else I should be doing?

Also, buy $MP.

### Post #61: 2025-06-03 22:53 — `$MP` `[News]`

**America’s Mineral Panic Button Has Been Hit**

Trump is set to use the Defense Production Act to waive legal requirements, including some congressional funding requirements, to fast-track production of critical minerals and weapons. Why? Because the clock is ticking and China’s rare-earth export licensing requirements (i.e., export ban) is already gripping supply chains by the throat. 

The real chess move is that China is requiring licenses of critical mineral exports to all countries, not just to the United States. A not so subtle reminder to the world as they look to renegotiate trade deals with the U.S. that China has the ability to cripple their economies by limiting access to rare earth exports. 

A repeat of the spectacular showing from Ukraine with the drone attacks in Russia. Not without China. 

Wind turbines spinning across the plains of Texas.
Not without China.

5G towers humming with encrypted comms across Europe. Not without China. 

Hypersonic missiles maneuvering at Mach 5 over the Taiwan Strait. Not without China. 

Quantum labs bending the laws of encryption in the Netherlands. Not without China. 

Modern infrastructure and economic stability now sit at the mercy of China.

Just last week automakers issued a blunt warning: production could halt within weeks. Not months. Weeks. The rare-earth magnet supply is drying up fast and there’s no plug-and-play replacement. No domestic cushion. No friendly supplier waiting in the wings. 

Many U.S.-based companies stand to benefit from Trump’s expected waiver of DPA requirements. From rare earth miners to critical metal processors, this move signals a fast-track lane for projects that have long been stalled by red tape and underfunding.

MP Materials ($MP) remains front and center for me but others in the space will see renewed attention as the U.S. scrambles to onshore supply chains and reduce dependency on China. 

Let’s be clear though. Despite my conviction for $MP, there is no immediate workaround. No silver bullet here. Cooperation with China remains essential. The real objective now is long-term resilience, not short-term bravado. Hopefully, Trump and his team understand the difference.

What tickers are you expecting to benefit from Trump’s actions tomorrow? Drop them in the comments.

### Post #67: 2025-06-05 23:04 — `$MP` `[Dd]`

**Gold in the front, REEs in the back. $DTREF is the mullet of mining plays.**

I mentioned Dateline Resources ($DTREF) in passing a couple weeks ago when I took a position. It wasn’t about its current gold deposits or any immediate catalyst. It was about the setup. At least one of you listened. Shout out to @GeneralMunchkin.

Dateline’s primary asset is the Colosseum Project in San Bernardino County, CA, a former-producing open pit gold mine with 1.1 million ounces of NI 43-101 resources. NI 43-101 is the globally recognized standard for independently verified mineral resource estimates. A recent scoping study, using $2,900 per ounce gold, values the project at $550 million NPV with a 61 percent pre-tax IRR. No debt. No royalties. Infrastructure already in place. At around $0.08 per share, the company’s market cap sits near $250 million, roughly 0.5 times NPV, which is about where you’d expect a U.S.-based pre-production gold developer.

That might be enough on its own. But the real reason to look closer is twofold. New data suggests the gold system is significantly larger than previously understood, and the rare earth element potential is still entirely unpriced.

In late May, Dateline reported geochemical sampling and mapping of felsite outcrops 200 to 900 meters west and southwest of the open pits. The samples showed elevated bismuth, tellurium, antimony, and arsenic, classic pathfinder elements found on the fringes of intrusion-related gold systems. Translation: Colosseum may host a much larger, vertically zoned gold system, with higher-grade zones at depth, well beyond the current 1.1 million ounces. Worth noting, all 641 historic drill holes only targeted exposed breccia pipes. Large portions of the property remain completely untested.

Structural mapping confirms that newly identified felsite dykes, vertical sheets of volcanic rock often associated with mineralization, align with known gold-bearing trends. The geological model is intact. This isn’t just surface noise. It’s a credible sign that more gold zones could be sitting just beyond the footprint mined in the past.

Meanwhile, Dateline is also advancing rare earth exploration. Monazite-bearing material has already been identified at surface. Colosseum is just six miles from MP Materials’ Mountain Pass mine, which houses the only REE separation facility in the United States. If Dateline defines economic REE mineralization, they may have a direct path to monetization by leveraging $MP’s existing infrastructure without having to build their own. That shifts the economics of the project materially.

They’ve also submitted documentation and meet eligibility for uplisting to OTCQB, which is expected soon. That matters. OTCQB requires audited financials, regulatory compliance, and improved disclosure. It expands brokerage access, opens the door to institutional participation, and typically improves liquidity. It doesn’t change the asset, but it tightens the gap between value and visibility.

So here’s the setup. DTREF is fairly valued on its current gold economics. But beneath the surface, both literally and strategically, the upside is stacking. A larger gold system is emerging. Rare earth exposure remains a free call option with a processing partner next door. And broader market access is around the corner.

I’m in because of what hasn’t been proven yet. And what they’re starting to find is pointing in the right direction.

This is a lotto ticket with a backstop, but there’s still risk. Position accordingly. I got in at three cents and still capped my allocation at five thousand dollars. I might have done a bit more if I wasn’t already balls deep in this sector with $MP. Sorry Robinhood users, but you can’t buy this ticker since they don’t support OTC trades.

### Post #70: 2025-06-08 13:40

**Ego is the Enemy - Book Recommendation**

The drama on AH this weekend has nothing to do with markets and everything to do with identity addiction. A need to be right, to be worshipped. People aren’t debating ideas, but defending reputations. 

That same mindset shows up in the worst trades:
	•	Holding losers to avoid admitting you’re wrong
	•	Doubling down on bias instead of adjusting to new data
	•	Confusing conviction with stubbornness
	•	Chasing clout over sharpening your edge

Ego is the Enemy by Ryan Holiday should be required reading. It’s a clear, focused look at how pride, insecurity, and overconfidence destroy performance, backed by stories from history, business, and sports. It’s my favorite non-investing investment book. 

If you manage risk this book belongs on your shelf. No matter how many investment books you read or how many strategies you study, if you can’t master yourself and your ego, you’ve already lost.

### Post #82: 2025-06-12 22:51 — `$ONDS` `[Discuss]`

**They knew. Why else would they not wait one more day?**

I’ve convinced myself that Brock and his executive team at $ONDS knew the Executive Order was coming.

Why else would you announce a dilution the day before you lock in Nasdaq compliance? Monday was day 9 above $1. One more to go. Instead, they drop a dilution after the close? Why? You’re basically daring the stock to hold the line on Tuesday.

And here’s the twist: it did.

Despite the after-hours selloff, $ONDS actually closed up from its open on Tuesday, securing that critical 10th day. Then on Wednesday, with the compliance box checked, it dropped hard closing 12 points down from open.

Makes you wonder: who was buying Tuesday to keep that floor intact? 

Then Wednesday after hours, the EO drops focused on domestic drone defense. Today, $ONDS rips. The dilution? Completely erased.

This was orchestration at its finest. 

They didn’t raise before the EO by accident, they created distance on purpose. Raising after the EO would’ve looked like they were cashing in on government news. And likely the stock would be far lower than where it finished today had the sequence been reversed. Instead, they took the hit early, let the selling shake out, and timed it so the pump looked pure.

They walked away with:

– Nasdaq compliance
– A completed raise
– Zero scrutiny

### Post #86: 2025-06-13 14:12 — `$TWOH` `[Dd]`

**Now’s the time to buy $TWOH if you’re gonna do it**

I posted about this ticker a few days ago. Wasn’t quite ready then, but now I’ve pulled the trigger. 

Earlier this week, $TWOH put out a press release doubling down on food services, new execs, ChefXperience relaunch, same story. Traders were expecting a merger bombshell and instead got a catering update. Price faded.

But then the .X disappeared on the CSE ticker. The .X on the CSE means the company is non-compliant, often flagged as a shell or inactive. As long as it’s there, the company is restricted, especially when it comes to mergers or big directional changes.

So why does this matter? Because announcing a merger or change of business while still wearing the .X tag would have immediately triggered a halt in trading and forced a full reapplication to the exchange. Shareholder votes, new filings, the whole thing. That would delay everything by months and freeze the stock.  

Instead, they removed the .X first by reviving the food business narrative enough to check the “active operations” box and stay in good standing. Now the ticker stays live, and they’re free to drop the real news at any time.

Oooh baby. This is about to get good. I’m in for 2 million shares. 

Follow @TimeisKey.

https://afterhour.com/TimeIsKey/Qji/dollartwoh

### Post #106: 2025-06-25 17:37 — `$PL, $BKSY` `[Dd]`

**Starting a position on $BKSY**

Been watching $BKSY for a few weeks now. Wish I had pulled the trigger earlier, but had other plays in motion. Not the best entry point here, so I’ll scale in slowly, targeting 3K to 5K shares. Sitting on 500 for now.

BlackSky operates in the geospatial intelligence space, using satellites and AI to deliver real-time imagery and analysis for defense, government, and commercial clients. There are a few players in this space, but $BKSY strikes the best balance of speed, capability, and future upside.

Maxar (now private) offers ultra-high-resolution imagery but suffers from slow revisit rates and weather limitations due to its optical-only approach, not ideal for real-time operations. Planet Labs ($PL) provides broad daily coverage with medium resolution but lacks SAR (radar that sees through clouds and darkness) and the real-time, AI-driven insights BlackSky delivers.

That’s where $BKSY stands out. It combines rapid-revisit optical satellites with SAR for true all-weather, day-and-night coverage. Its Spectra AI platform turns raw data into actionable intelligence within minutes, key for battlefield awareness, targeting, and fast-moving missions where timing is everything.

Earlier this year, they began launching their Gen-3 satellites, now live with 35 cm resolution and even faster revisit times. Two are already in orbit with more coming this year, expanding global coverage and spectral capabilities.

Beyond defense, BlackSky’s platform is built for disaster response, infrastructure monitoring, and supply chain visibility. Areas with growing demand for real-time insight. Only about 3% of 2024 revenue came from non-defense, but they’re actively growing that side through subscriptions and leveraging Gen‑3’s enhanced data and analytics.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/Rpu/starting-a-position-on-dollarb*

### Post #108: 2025-06-26 17:35 `[Discuss]`

**You don’t get all three**

Time, direction, and magnitude.
Everyone wants to be right on all three. That’s the fantasy.

One trader hears a rumor and loads weekly calls way OTM.
He’s sure it’s going up, and fast, maybe a double by Friday.
He’s playing prophet: trying to guess when, which way, and how far. It almost works… until it doesn’t.

Another trader watches the same setup, she nods at the direction, sizes in gradually. Doesn’t need the perfect entry, doesn’t care if it takes weeks. She doesn’t know the magnitude just that the reward skews far enough. No hero trades. No Hail Marys. Just probability stacking.

Pick two. Respect the third.

That’s how you stay in the game and eventually, win it.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/RvI/you-dont-get-all-three*

### Post #117: 2025-07-06 01:59 `[Dd]`

**The Unwritten**


Nobody notices them at first. They slip across the early sky while we sip coffee and scroll headlines.

A child vanishes on her way to school. Before fear even takes its first breath, they’re already moving, sweeping parks, tracing footprints in dew-soaked fields, seeing what no human could see. She is found safe before a headline ever has a chance to form.

A bridge holds thousands on their daily commute. Above it, they watch endlessly, sensing fractures thinner than a hair. Disasters that would once dominate front pages never happen.

A spark leaps in a dry hillside. Before sirens wail, they are already drawing escape routes, guiding first responders, marking invisible fire lines on digital maps. A town is protected before flames ever reach its edge.

A stadium pulses with noise and adrenaline. A loud crack. Panic ripples. They see every surge, every blocked exit, every terrified face and orchestrate a calm, precise exit that turns a potential headline tragedy into a footnote of relief.

They’re not just machines of war. They’re silent architects of normal days. Guardians of routines we never realize almost didn’t happen. They protect the stories we never have to tell.

The market will reward those who can see the stories that will remain unwritten. 

https://event.choruscall.com/mediaframe/webcast.html?webcastid=3EIL7ZLv

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/ShH/the-unwritten*

Solid. Clear pivot to execution and revenue with no new greenfield distractions, which is music to my ears from a capital standpoint. Strong Q2 revenue jump (6x YoY) and $23M backlog validate that this is moving beyond R&D talk and into real deployments.

I like the M&A strategy: small, targeted bolt-ons to deepen the OAS stack rather than big, dilutive headline deals. Emphasis on building a unified platform instead of a patchwork of tech pieces. That’s easier said than done, but the right approach imo.

$67M cash on hand and materially reduced debt give them flexibility to scale without immediate dilution. If they execute on backlog and margin lift as hinted, there’s no near-term need for additional capital outside of how they would leverage Klear.

Networks is clearly taking a back seat and OAS is now the full story. Execution from here will determine whether they can justify the premium multiple.

We should hear initial reactions from analysts tomorrow and Friday with full write-ups next week. Hopefully we see some upward price targets from them. 🤞🤞🤞

### Post #130: 2025-08-06 22:20 — `$ONDS` `[Discuss]`

**100 baggers**

I was listening to the latest AfterWhores podcast today where the Whores were talking about 100x tickers. Coincidently I’m currently reading 100 Baggers: Stocks That Return 100-to-1 and How to Find Them by Christopher Mayer. Before you get too excited, 100 baggers typically take a decade or longer to play out. 

For the book he did a look-back at prior 100 baggers to establish common criteria for identifying them. Here’s what the book says:

•	Low starting market cap
→ Smaller companies have more room to grow.

•	Long runway for growth
→ Expanding industries, scalable business models, or global markets.

•	High return on equity (ROE)
→ Often above 15% and sustained over years.

•       Consistent earnings growth
→ Not just a one-time spike, compounding growth is crucial.

•	Owner-operator mentality
→ Founder-led or major insider ownership aligns incentives.

•	Reasonable valuation at entry
→ Avoid overpaying, even for high-growth companies.

•	Strong and durable competitive advantage (moat)
→ Brand, network effects, patents, scale, etc.

•	Margin expansion potential
→ Room to improve profitability as it scales.

•	Reinvestment opportunities
→ Company can reinvest profits at high returns internally.

•	Time + patience
→ Most 100 baggers took 15–30 years. 

As an example, let’s look at $ONDS which I think has a legitimate chance of being a 100 bagger. It doesn’t seem crazy to me that $ONDS becomes a $50B+ company in a decade or two. 

Here’s where $ONDS hits Mayer’s criteria: low market cap, growth runway, competitive moat , margin potential, insider ownership, and reinvestment capacity. 

High ROE, earnings growth, and valuation aren’t there…yet. These attributes will play out (or not) over the next few years as $ONDS moves from R&D to execution, ramping sales and reducing overhead. 

The book is a good read with some great examples to add context to the criteria, so would recommend reading it despite this summary post. 

You got any future 100x tickers that are checking all these boxes? Let’s find ‘em. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/V8m/100-baggers*

Now overlay that onto what Ondas already has: aerial autonomy running on wide-area wireless networks.

### Post #145: 2025-09-04 19:52 `[News]`

**I think the Venezuelan drug boat footage was fabricated**

But I’m not sure it matters. 

Trump’s second term is shaping up to be focused on the re-engineering of American power to reclaim American supremacy on the international stage. What looks like chaos (tariffs on China, cartel designations, withdrawal from global compacts, now a strike on a Venezuelan “drug boat”) is actually a unified strategy: dismantle the liberal international order, strip away the constraints of law, and remind the world that American dominance rests not on consensus, but on the spectacle of force.

The boat matters less than what it symbolizes. If it was fabricated (as Venezuela claims) it reveals the administration’s comfort with constructing reality outright. If it was real, it shows the willingness to cross into extrajudicial killings in international waters, a war crime under any sober reading of international law. Both outcomes point to the same truth: Supremacy doesn’t recognize law, only performance. This is Gulf of Tonkin energy repackaged for the age of social media, with missiles staged for the feed.

Here’s what I expect is next. Congressional hearings where the White House justifies military strikes under the cover of “terrorist designations.” Expect a buildup of U.S. naval power in the Caribbean, framed as counter-narco patrols but clearly aimed at Venezuela. Expect more military “demonstrations,” because Trump governs through spectacle, and spectacle demands repetition. Maduro will respond with militias and defiance, but Washington has already written the script: the regime is the enemy, and the doctrine is escalation.

And the dot almost no one connects: by labeling cartels as terrorists and then striking them with military force, Trump collapses the boundary between “domestic crime” and “foreign war.” That legal bridge is what enables the Pentagon, not DEA or Coast Guard, to seize the mandate. Once established, that doctrine won’t stop at Venezuela. It will extend to Ecuador, Colombia, Mexico, even the Caribbean shipping lanes. We are watching in real time the creation of a new hemispheric security order where the U.S. military replaces multilateral institutions as the arbiter of stability. This is Monroe Doctrine 2.0, enforced not with diplomacy but with drone footage.

Capital follows power. An American supremacy doctrine means defense becomes structural, commodities become unstable, emerging markets become collateral damage, and U.S. domestics consolidate the premium. The “brilliance” of the doctrine is that the spectacle itself generates the flows that reinforce American strength. The stronger the display, the more the world retreats into the dollar, into Treasuries, into U.S. assets. Supremacy is self-funding.

The trades: stay long U.S. defense contractors as secular winners; position for energy and commodity volatility; steer away from emerging markets, particularly in Latin America; and overweight U.S.-centric equities (Russell 2000 small caps, domestic banks, infrastructure plays) that capture capital fleeing instability.

Even though the play is the same, I can’t help but wonder which is better… the total fabrication of an event to justify further military action or the U.S. perpetrating war crimes on a self-released video? Tough one.

### Post #149: 2025-09-08 23:03

**Eels are f’n wild**

Eels are unlike anything else. Aristotle thought they rose from mud (and people believed him for centuries). Freud cut through hundreds of them searching for sex organs and found nothing. To this day, no one has seen them reproduce. Ever. And we really don’t even know how they do it. 

However, we do know that every eel on the planet spawns at the same place. When they determine it’s time to spawn, they just disappear into the Atlantic, into the Sargasso Sea, and months later the rivers are full again. We’ve even tried to track this movement with satellites, unsuccessfully. Proof without witness.

They live in stages. Glass. Elver. Yellow. Silver. Each form unrecognizable from the last, yet all the same creature. They can pause for decades in stasis without reproducing, until the signal comes. Then the body transforms: eyes widen, organs dissolve, muscles harden. They swim thousands of miles guided only by the Earth’s magnetic field. They spawn once, with total commitment, and die.

Every eel begins in surrender…tiny, transparent, helpless… carried across oceans by currents it cannot control, delivered blindly to the rivers of its kind. But what starts as surrender ends as conviction. The same creature that once drifted without direction will one day transform and swim back thousands of miles with perfect certainty, pulled only by an invisible compass toward its final act.

A good investor’s journey is the same. At the start, you’re carried by the current, chasing noise, drifting with the crowd, blindly following AH grifters, not yet in control. But over time you transform. You learn patience: the strength to wait without forcing the trade. You learn to evolve with cycles: adapting, shedding old forms without losing your core. You learn conviction: to move without a map, guided by your own compass. And when the signal finally comes, you learn decisiveness, no hesitation, no half-measures, just total commitment. Like an eel.

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/Zfq/eels-are-fn-wild*

### Post #154: 2025-09-18 14:12 `[News]`

**$HUMA Flywheel**

$HUMA announced preclinical results today for its coronary tissue engineered vessel (CTEV) in coronary artery bypass grafting (CABG). In a six-month primate study, every graft remained open, became repopulated with the host’s own cells, and remodeled toward native vessel dimensions. The company plans to file an Investigational New Drug (IND) application in Q4 2025 to begin first-in-human CABG studies.

The science is encouraging, but the strategy may be even more important: Humacyte is building a flywheel.

Amazon popularized this concept. A flywheel starts with heavy effort, but each rotation builds momentum, creating a self-reinforcing loop. In Amazon’s case, lower prices drove more customers, which attracted more sellers, which increased selection, which further improved the customer experience. That loop kept compounding.

Humacyte’s version looks like this:
	•	Single manufacturing platform produces vessels of different sizes (large-diameter for trauma, medium for dialysis access, small-diameter for coronary use).
	•	Regulatory precedent: FDA approval of the acellular tissue engineered vessel (ATEV) for vascular trauma in 2024 makes each subsequent indication easier to evaluate and approve.
	•	Clinical credibility: Data from each program reinforces confidence across the pipeline. Dialysis access, peripheral arterial disease (PAD), and now CABG.
	•	Market expansion: Trauma is a wedge market; dialysis and PAD add scale; CABG is the crown jewel with massive patient need.
	•	Margin leverage: Every new use case spreads fixed costs across more volume, lowers cost per unit, and boosts margins across the entire platform.

What makes this powerful is not just the potential of the coronary program, but the compounding effect of the model itself. Each success doesn’t stand alone but it accelerates the next. With every new indication, Humacyte gains scale, reduces costs, and strengthens clinical and regulatory confidence. That is the essence of a true flywheel: a platform that grows faster, stronger, and more profitable the longer it spins.

I’m still sitting on 50K shares I bought earlier this year. I also picked up 300 options yesterday that are expiring in December at $1.50 strike.

### Post #158: 2025-09-22 23:13 — `$NRGV` `[Dd]`

**Gravity, Batteries, and a Billion-Dollar Backlog**

When the sun sets and the wind stalls, grids strain, blackouts threaten, and fossil plants fire back up. Without storage, renewables plateau. With it, the grid becomes flexible, reliable, and clean.

That need has created one of the largest infrastructure build-outs of our era. Energy Vault ($NRGV) is a small-cap name positioning itself right at that inflection point.

People that know the company, know it for its gravity towers (a build out in China pictured), massive blocks stacked and lowered to mimic pumped hydro where dams can’t be built. But the real story is more practical and, frankly, more investable: Energy Vault builds grid-scale battery projects and runs them with its software platform, VaultOS. They’ve already delivered over a gigawatt-hour of capacity in the U.S., the type of assets utilities lean on for evening peaks and resilience.

VaultOS is the brain. A tech-agnostic control system that can orchestrate gravity towers, lithium-ion batteries, and even hydrogen storage under one interface. It forecasts demand, arbitrages prices, and dispatches storage in real time, turning physical assets into recurring revenue streams. Every project Energy Vault delivers is also a software customer… and software margins compound.

The investment case:
	•	Backlog vs. valuation: Nearly $1B in contracted projects vs. a ~$500M market cap. Disconnect is obvious.
	•	Revenue model pivot: Once an EPC contractor, now shifting to holding projects on balance sheet. Near-term reported revenue dipped in 2024, but long-term contracted cash flows are rising. Gross margins are already improving.
	•	Insider conviction: The CEO and co-founder just bought 50,000 shares on the open market. Skin in the game.

Energy storage isn’t optional, every grid operator is procuring it, policy tailwinds are aligned, and the demand curve is only steepening. The question isn’t if storage gets built, but who gets paid to build and operate it.

$NRGV is up nearly 50% in the last week and 100% in the last month, still sitting just under a $500M market cap. Plenty of runway, though short-term volatility is inevitable. Dilution risk is real if revenue lags, but the setup for the back half of the year looks strong.

I don’t have much dry powder right now, but I picked up 1,000 shares today. And not to brag, but I’m already up $0.30.

Trending Sector – Ride the waves where policy, demand, and narrative converge. Usually hold a few weeks to a few months depending on the narrative and price action. ($MP, $BKSY, $DTREF)

Income / Yield – Wheel, ATM covered calls, synthetic dividends. This is not where I like to focus but currently trading is the primary source of income for me so I mix these in as a bit of guaranteed income. ($MSTY, $ULTY) 

Hype / Speculation – Pure sentiment and timing. Smallest allocation by far. ($CLBR/$PEW, $TWOH, $SQQQ for Liberation Day)

### Post #178: 2025-10-13 23:07 `[Discuss]`

**Maybe.**

I’ve always liked “The Story of the Chinese Farmer” by Alan Watts. I try to remember it whenever something in life (or investing) goes south.

Earlier this year I sold 1,000 $2 puts on a ticker. Two hours later, they announced a dilution. Brutal timing. A couple weeks later, I was the proud owner of 100,000 shares at $2. At the time, it felt like bad luck.

Four months later, those shares were worth over $1M. Shitty luck, huh? 😂

Hope this little parable helps someone out there trying to make sense of their own timing or “bad luck”. 

“Once upon a time there was a Chinese farmer whose horse ran away. That evening, all of his neighbors came around to commiserate. They said, ‘We are so sorry to hear your horse has run away. This is most unfortunate.’ The farmer said, ‘Maybe.’

The next day the horse came back bringing seven wild horses with it, and in the evening everybody came back and said, ‘Oh, isn’t that lucky.’ The farmer again said, ‘Maybe.’

The following day his son tried to break one of the horses, and while riding it, he was thrown and broke his leg. The neighbors then said, ‘Oh dear, that’s too bad,’ and the farmer responded, ‘Maybe.’

The next day the conscription officers came to recruit soldiers, but rejected his son because he had a broken leg. The neighbors said, ‘Isn’t that great!’ Again, he said, ‘Maybe.’

“The whole process of nature is an integrated process of immense complexity, and it’s really impossible to tell whether anything that happens is good or bad — because you never know what will be the consequence of the misfortune; or, you never know what will be the consequences of good fortune.”

— Alan Watts

### Post #182: 2025-10-15 02:06 — `$XOM, $SOC` `[Dd]`

**The Santa Ynez Saga**

Off the coast of Santa Barbara sits one of California’s last great oil frontiers, the Santa Ynez Unit. Three towering offshore platforms that once pumped tens of thousands of barrels a day into the state’s energy grid.

For decades, these rigs were $XOM’s pride. Then in 2015 a corroded pipeline ruptured, spilling oil onto Refugio Beach and handing anti-energy activists the ammunition they’d waited decades for. The line was shut down. Exxon was buried under lawsuits, protests, and politics.

Fast-forward seven years. Exxon gives up. In 2022, they sell the entire system to a small operator called Sable Offshore Corp ($SOC) for $643 million. The bet was that someone with more patience and less corporate baggage could do what Big Oil couldn’t. Restart Santa Ynez. 

But there’s a catch. The deal included a deadline: if Sable doesn’t restart production by March 1, 2026, Exxon can take it all back for free. Every delay inches them closer to that cliff. And delay is exactly what Santa Barbara does best.

Today, a local court issued another temporary judgment, freezing Sable’s restart, the latest stunt in a long line of bureaucratic ambushes dressed up as “environmental review.” It’s not about the planet; it’s about politics.

The oil is still there. The infrastructure is still there. The only thing standing between $SOC and hundreds of millions in annual cash flow is a signature.

If $SOC keeps getting bogged down, bet on the Karens of Santa Barbara to outlast the clock. But if the winds shift… perhaps a presidential directive reframes this as a national energy-security issue. That’s when it gets interesting.

“Energy is a national priority. California can’t keep blocking progress. Newsom is clueless. Drill, baby, drill.” You can hear the sound bites already. 

If that does happen, the re-rate could be violent:
	•	20–50 K barrels/day potential
	•	Hundreds of millions in EBITDA
	•	3–5× market cap expansion as a “dead asset” suddenly comes alive

Overnight. 

For now, I’m not rushing in. I think this still has room to drop. But I do think this stock has an interesting story and the next chapter could be written in DC, not Santa Barbara. 

*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/5IH/the-santa-ynez-saga*

### Post #189: 2025-10-21 20:09 — `$HUMA` `[Dd]`

**$HUMA Series Post 1: The Company + The Machine**

Humacyte ($HUMA) sits at the intersection of biotech and industrial manufacturing. At the moment where tissue engineering stops being theory and becomes infrastructure. The company’s mission is simple to say but hard to do: grow human tissue at industrial scale.

Its first product, SYMVESS™, is the first FDA-approved human acellular vessel for urgent arterial repair when a patient’s own vein isn’t usable. The graft is shelf-stable, sterile, and ready for immediate implantation. 

That last sentence represents two decades of work. Humacyte learned how to grow human vascular tissue around a biodegradable scaffold seeded with donor smooth-muscle cells. After the structure forms, the cells are washed away, leaving a collagen-based framework that a patient’s own cells later repopulate. The result functions like a living vein but avoids the infection, rejection, and operative risk of synthetics or patient-derived grafts.

But the vessel itself is only the first proof point. The real moat is the machine that makes it.

LUNA200 — The Factory for Living Tissues

Inside Humacyte’s 83,000 sq ft Durham, NC facility runs a fully operational, automated biomanufacturing platform called LUNA200.

Each LUNA200 module is a closed, computer-controlled bioreactor built for GMP (Good Manufacturing Practice). GMP is the FDA standard for reproducibility, sterility, and auditability. Every environmental variable (temperature, pH, nutrient flow, oxygen, shear stress) is digitally monitored and logged to ensure consistency.

At the heart of each module is a proprietary drawer system, now protected by patent through 2040. Each drawer acts as its own growth chamber: a scaffolded cylinder is perfused with culture medium and gently rotated to mimic arterial stress. The drawers slide in and out like trays in a reactor bank, enabling continuous, parallel production with minimal downtime.

One unit can grow roughly 200 vessels per run; multiple modules run in parallel, giving current capacity to exceed 40,000 grafts per year. LUNA200 converts what used to be artisanal lab work into an industrial process, the tissue-engineering equivalent of a semiconductor fab line.

One Platform, Many Products

LUNA200 isn’t a single-product tool. It’s the platform that manufactures every Human Acellular Vessel (HAV) in Humacyte’s pipeline:
	•	SYMVESS™ (Trauma) – 6 mm vessels for emergency arterial repair
	•	AV Access (Dialysis) – 6 mm grafts optimized for vascular access creation
	•	PAD (Peripheral Artery Disease) – smaller-diameter conduits in development
	•	CABG (Cardio Bypass) – 3–4 mm vessels in pre-clinical work

Because all products share the same cell-growth process, the factory simply adjusts scaffold diameter and run time, no hardware change needed.  Once validated under GMP, each new indication benefits from the same manufacturing license and quality system.  Scale in one line lowers cost across them all.

The Long-Game — From Vessels to Organs

Humacyte’s platform doesn’t stop at arteries.  The same decellularized-scaffold method can be adapted for tubular and hollow organs, trachea, ureter, even small-diameter bile ducts and sections of intestine, all of which rely on similar biological mechanics.

CEO Laura Niklason’s team has already published early pre-clinical data showing HAV-derived constructs used in arteriovenous loops to pre-vascularize organ scaffolds.  In the long run, the company’s expertise in growing perfusable vascular networks positions it to become the supply chain for whole-organ biofabrication, an essential piece of regenerative medicine that every organ-printing or tissue-engineering effort will eventually need.

LUNA200 is therefore more than a vessel factory; it’s the prototype for industrial-scale organ engineering.  The infrastructure being validated today for trauma and dialysis could ultimately serve as the backbone for producing life-supporting human tissues tomorrow.

Humacyte’s science created SYMVESS. Its LUNA200 platform makes it scalable. Together they transform regenerative medicine from a boutique science into a manufacturable industry and open the door to something larger: a world where organs are grown, not donated.

### Post #191: 2025-10-22 11:46 — `$HUMA` `[Dd]`

**$HUMA Series Post 2: Pricing Power & Hospital Economics**

SYMVESS is priced around $24K per graft. At face value that seems high, but in vascular surgery, price isn’t the same as cost. Outcomes dictate the total economics.

Hospitals evaluate new products through total cost of care, not unit price. A single vascular trauma or dialysis graft failure can easily run $50K - $100K once you add infection treatment, re-operations, and extended hospital stays.

Synthetic grafts like ePTFE (expanded polytetrafluoroethylene) have dominated for decades because they’re cheap and shelf-ready. But they fail frequently, especially in contaminated trauma fields. They’re inert, prone to infection, and they don’t integrate with the body.

Patency measures how long a graft remains open and functioning, the defining test of long-term success in vascular medicine. Primary patency means continuous flow without interruption, while secondary patency includes brief touch-ups to keep it open. The better the patency, the fewer complications, infections, and hospital stays and the lower the overall cost per patient.

Decades of data show ePTFE grafts struggle in contaminated trauma settings, with early occlusion and infection rates often exceeding 30%, while SYMVESS held 66.7% primary and 72.2% secondary patency at 30 days in extremity trauma, a durability record synthetic materials have never matched.

SYMVESS is biologic, not synthetic. It behaves like living tissue, resisting infection and gradually becomes part of the patient’s own vasculature. In clinical studies, its patency rates have approached those of self-derived veins, far exceeding ePTFE. That means every implanted SYMVESS stays open longer and demands fewer downstream interventions translating directly into savings.

Humacyte lowered the price from roughly $29K to $24K in 2025 to streamline hospital VAC approvals and eliminate budget friction. That adjustment helps turn the conversation from price to proof. At $24K, the graft typically models as cost-neutral or cost-saving when hospitals account for OR time, infection prevention, and reduced revisions.

The real pricing power isn’t in charging more but it’s in proving that a higher-performance biologic reduces total system cost. Once those economics are validated at scale, reimbursement will follow automatically, and gross margins will expand through LUNA200 efficiency.

SYMVESS doesn’t compete on price. It competes on patency, safety, and permanence. And I think that’s exactly how you win in medicine.

### Post #192: 2025-10-23 09:30 — `$HUMA` `[Dd]`

**$HUMA Series Post 3: ECAT, VA, & DoD Network**

ECAT (Electronic Catalog) is the Defense Logistics Agency’s secure procurement marketplace for approved medical devices and supplies. If a product is on ECAT, any military treatment facility can order it directly. No bids, no new contracts, no local purchasing red tape.

After FDA approval, SYMVESS was added to the ECAT listing, making it available across roughly 35 military hospitals and over 150 VA facilities. This matters because ECAT listings are demand-driven, not speculative. Once a product is on catalog, every DoD trauma surgeon, vascular specialist, or logistics officer can requisition it immediately.

Why the Government Channel Matters
	1.	Credibility.
Getting through DoD procurement is its own validation step. The government’s technical evaluation replaces the “trust gap” that often slows civilian adoption.
	2.	Data.
Every use of SYMVESS in the military or VA system is documented and reportable. Those cases create the early real-world evidence that drives reimbursement and future indications.
	3.	Volume Base.
Even modest penetration in DoD and VA facilities generates recurring baseline demand. It’s a durable, recession-proof customer base that helps stabilize early cash flow.
	4.	Strategic Visibility.
The DoD’s investment in battlefield medicine aligns perfectly with Humacyte’s mission. SYMVESS was developed under DARPA funding to treat vascular trauma in combat. The government connection is $HUMA’s origin story.

The Knock-On Effect

In med-tech adoption, military validation often precedes civilian acceptance. The same thing happened with negative-pressure wound therapy, tourniquets, and advanced hemostatics. Once the DoD proves something works in the harshest settings on earth, Level-1 trauma centers follow.

That’s exactly what’s starting to happen with Humacyte. The ECAT listing created instant logistical access; now surgeon experience is building the clinical proof. As those cases publish, civilian VAC committees will move faster and not out of marketing, but out of precedent.

SYMVESS doesn’t need a massive sales force to get into hospitals. It can ride in through the front door… through ECAT, through the VA, and through the Defense Department’s demand for solutions that work when nothing else does.

### Post #196: 2025-10-24 12:32 — `$HUMA` `[Dd]`

**$HUMA Series Post 4: FDA Approval and the Storm to Follow**

On December 19, 2024, the U.S. Food and Drug Administration granted full approval for SYMVESS, Humacyte’s acellular, tissue-engineered human vessel, for use in vascular trauma when autologous veins aren’t available and urgent revascularization is required. It was the first product of its kind ever cleared, a vessel grown from human cells, decellularized, and shelf-stable for immediate surgical use. 

The milestone should’ve been a coronation. Instead, it came wrapped in noise. Inside the FDA, a few internal reviewers argued the agency had moved too quickly. The loudest was Dr. Robert E. Lee (yep, real name), a vascular surgeon who resigned after the decision, calling the pivotal data incomplete. A few months later, a citizen petition emerged from a Northeastern University research group demanding the FDA revoke the approval, a move that generated headlines and social-media outrage but, critically, contained no new data or evidence of harm. Even Martin Shkreli jumped in, telling traders to short the stock and claiming the product “would never sell.”

For a few months, that narrative defined the conversation. Then the facts reasserted themselves.

Humacyte’s management responded decisively. Dr. Laura Niklason, the company’s founder and CEO, clarified that SYMVESS underwent a full Biologics License Application review, site inspections, and post-approval surveillance commitments like any other Class III product. The FDA backed that up with its actions, or lack thereof really. The agency declined to reopen or review the approval, effectively dismissing the petition. There was no recall, no advisory committee, no modification of the label. 

By mid-2025, the “rushed approval” story had faded. No adverse signals had surfaced. The Defense Logistics Agency added SYMVESS to its ECAT procurement catalog, giving U.S. military trauma surgeons direct access to the product. Hospital Value Analysis Committees that once hesitated have begun approving its use, and early post-approval feedback from trauma teams mirrors what the clinical trials showed: a biologic conduit that handles like native tissue, resists infection, and performs under pressure.

For investors, the very controversy that crushed sentiment has created one of the most asymmetric setups in med-tech. The stock is down more than 60% year-to-date, not because the thesis broke, but because the market priced in fear that never materialized. The petition failed, the FDA stood firm, and the product is already in the field.

Many innovations go through a phase like this, the growing pains where data collides with disbelief, and change feels threatening until it’s undeniable. That’s what makes this opportunity so compelling now. You’re looking at a company that just achieved something no one else in regenerative medicine has, full FDA approval of a lab-grown human vessel, while trading like it’s still waiting for clearance. 

The storm and fallout post-approval was a distraction. The real story is that SYMVESS works, it’s real, and it’s now officially in surgeons’ hands.

### Post #198: 2025-10-25 13:08 — `$HUMA` `[Dd]`

**$HUMA Series Post 5: AV Access: The Billion-Dollar Second Act**

Humacyte ($HUMA) has shown that a lab-grown human vessel can work in chaos through SYMVESS. But trauma is episodic. To build permanence, the same platform needs to tackle a problem that never ends. One of those problems is dialysis.

More than half a million Americans rely on hemodialysis three times a week. Each session depends on a surgical implanted arteriovenous access that lets blood flow rapidly through the machine. Autologous fistulas, the surgeon-made connections between a patient’s own artery and vein, are considered the current gold standard, but nearly half fail to mature or stay open long enough for regular dialysis. Synthetic grafts, made from ePTFE plastic, clog and infect constantly. Every failure means another operation, another hospitalization, another shot at sepsis.

Humacyte’s AV Access graft replaces plastic with biology. It’s grown from human smooth-muscle cells on a dissolving scaffold, then decellularized to leave a framework the body adopts as its own. Once implanted, the patient’s cells repopulate it, turning it into living tissue that heals, resists infection, and endures like a native vessel. Just like SYMVESS responds for trauma use. 

The results are striking. Humacyte grafts have shown 60–70 percent patency at one year, many lasting three to six years, some beyond seven. Infection rates are dramatically lower. Once the vessel matures, it behaves like a patient’s own vein, stronger, cleaner, built to last.

Durability is the strategy. A graft that lasts years doesn’t reduce revenue; it locks in the market. Hospitals back what works. Payers reward what saves money. The patient base refreshes every year as new dialysis cases come online. Stability, not churn, is the foundation for scale.

By late 2024, the FDA approval of SYMVESS had already set the regulatory blueprint. Through 2025, Humacyte has been gathering long-term dialysis data across high-risk groups, such as pregnant women. The company expects to file for FDA approval in the second half of 2026. Why so far off? As a biologic, the graft must meet FDA requirements for two-year durability data, 18-month stability testing, and full-scale manufacturing validation at Humacyte’s Durham facility. 

Dialysis isn’t episodic. It’s constant, reimbursed, and enormous, a $1–2 billion U.S. market alone where reliability wins every time. It isn’t heroic medicine. It’s routine, mechanical, relentless, the steady grind of keeping people alive one treatment at a time. That’s what makes this work so significant. A living vessel built for repetition, for endurance, for the parts of medicine that never make headlines. Not innovation for innovation’s sake, but repair made real. Something that doesn’t just keep the body going, but makes the system itself a little more human.
