**$HUMA Hemodialysis Trial**

$HUMA reports top-line interim data from its V012 Phase 3 trial on June 11 at the Society for Vascular Surgery meeting in Boston. It covers the first 80 patients at one-year follow-up, testing their bioengineered vessel against surgically created fistulas in women with end-stage kidney disease who need dialysis.

Good results support a supplemental approval filing with the FDA in the second half of 2026 to add dialysis access as a second use, beyond the already-approved Symvess product for traumatic injuries.

A fistula is the current standard of care for dialysis access. Humacyte’s pitch is that its vessel could become a preferred alternative for patients who can’t get a working fistula.

This is the near-term cash cow for $HUMA and why I’m stacking 2028 options.

### Post #425: 2026-06-09 22:54 — `$ONDS`

**My favorite irrelevant stock**

Someone asked me today if $ONDS is still relevant. Someone else asked why I have so much love for $ONDS. Let’s explore. 

Q1 2026 revenue was $50.1M, up 10x year over year and 66% from the prior quarter, beating the top of guidance by 25%. Management raised full-year 2026 guidance from $170-180M to at least $390M, roughly 670% growth. Gross margin went from 35% a year ago to 49%, and the product businesses hit adjusted-EBITDA positive about six months early. Backlog went from $68.3M at year-end to $457M at March 31, with another $110M-plus in new bookings already in Q2.

And I said this months ago, but despite all this explosive growth, Brock is sandbagging the guidance, and he will raise it again every quarter this year. Write that shit down.

So if the business is this strong, what’s been pushing the stock down?

Supply, not fundamentals. Mistral ($175M), Omnisys ($196.6M), and World View ($150M) were all paid largely in stock, and the company has been steadily registering those acquisition shares for resale. That’s real new float hitting the market right as the stock ran, and the dilution is a genuine piece of the pullback. But it’s the same stock that bought the $457M backlog, Mistral’s prime contractor access, Omnisys’s battlefield software, and World View’s stratospheric ISR. Not all dilution is bad dilution, but the market often treats it the same. 

The rest of the noise is the insider “selling” everyone on X keeps pointing at. On June 1, 4.5M of Brock’s RSUs vested, and the next day he sold ~2.4M shares at $13.43 to cover the tax withholding. A $32M tax bill, settled the way every RSU vest gets settled. 

So let’s get the bear case straight. A company growing revenue 670% diluted shareholders to buy a $457M backlog and a US defense prime, and its CEO can’t cover a $32M tax bill out of his savings account, so he must know the company is quickly moving to irrelevance. Got it. 

So what do you think? Is $ONDS irrelevant? Or is this recent dip an opportunity to buy a neo-prime at a steep discount? 


*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/bqA2/my-favorite-irrelevant-stock*

### Post #443: 2026-08-11 16:20 — `$ONDS`

**Been a busy quarter for $ONDS**

Guidance will increase…again. Last update was $525M rev for FY26. My guess is $625M. 

- May 18: Agreed to acquire Omnisys (battlefield orchestration software) for ~$197M in stock. Closed May 21.
- May 18: Proxy push for annual meeting quorum, slate included raising authorized shares from 800M to 1.2B.
- May 29: $30M+ in May orders, Q2-to-date above $110M, pro forma backlog ~$457M.
- Jun 2: World View selected by SOUTHCOM, 4th Fleet and SMX for maritime domain awareness. ~$4.8M initial three-month contract.
- Jun 10 / Jun 16: LADOS command-and-control layer plus a broader systems-of-systems launch at Eurosatory under "Autonomy at First Contact."
- Jun 18: Agreed to acquire Cyberhawk for ~$125M, mostly cash. $45M+ FY27 revenue, ~95% recurring, $95M backlog.
- Jun 22: $40M+ in June orders, lifting Q2 orders above $150M.
- Jun 23: Sentrycs Cyber-over-RF to be integrated into Lockheed Martin's Sanctum C-UAS platform.
- Jul 6 (closed Jul 2): Acquired DZYNE for ~$876M in cash and stock. ~$191M 2026 revenue, $300M+ in 2027, EBITDA positive. Creates the Ondas Sentinel division with World View.
- Jul 6: FY26 revenue target raised to at least $525M from at least $390M. Excludes Cyberhawk.
- Jul 20: $6.9M Australian Department of Defence order for DZYNE DTIM counter-sUAS kits.
- Jul 22: $70M in new orders over four weeks across ground, border, C-UAS, ISR and precision strike.
- Jul 24: Co-led a strategic investment with RSE Ventures in FPF Defense, low-cost C-UAS interceptors.
- Aug 3: Former Mossad Director David Barnea joined as Global President and Chairman of Ondas Defense.
- Aug 5: Mistral secured a $50M+ U.S. Army order for lethal unmanned systems under the $982M LUS IDIQ.
- Aug 6: AFRL award of more than $6M for the DZYNE LongRange Grasshopper aerial delivery system.
- Aug 7: Sentrycs selected for counter-drone coverage at Jacksonville Jaguars home games.
- Aug 10: Cyberhawk closed.
- Aug 11: Israeli Minister of Defense tender win, "Digital Bat," next-gen low-cost tactical attack drones. Multi-million dollar, size undisclosed.

### Post #445: 2026-08-13 17:16

**$ONDS Q2 Earnings Report Card**

Four things matter for $ONDS earnings. Ignore the rest. 

1. Revenue - Grade: A
$83.8M vs ~$68M consensus. Up 67% sequentially, 13x year over year, 85% organic on a same-portfolio basis. Raised the year to $525-550M, guided Q3 to $140-155M. You don’t guide up after a bad quarter.

2. Margin - Grade: A-
Adjusted gross margin 50.4% vs 51.5% in Q1. Flat while volume grew two-thirds, incremental margin around 49%. That answers a big question: does the backlog convert at decent economics? It does. The 43.1% in the headlines is intellectual property amortization running through cost of goods sold. Non-cash. Ignore. 

3. Growth - Grade: A
Backlog $613M reported, $757M pro forma, from $68M at year end 2025. $175M in new orders during Q2, another $105M already captured in Q3. Best part of this release. 

4. Cash Burn - Grade: C
Adjusted cash operating expense went $36.9M to $93.2M. Adjusted EBITDA loss $50.6M vs $10.9M. Q2 was guided as the peak loss quarter and it was. Street had cash opex near $50-55M and that got nearly doubled. Most of the increase looks like acquired cost from acquisitions rather than new spend. 10-Q may help answer this. 

I could see a grade below C based on the numbers but because they pulled profitability guidance forward while printing their worst burn quarter, I think a C is appropriate. Nobody does that with loose costs. 

Overall: B+. 
Three of four areas did about as well as they could. The fourth is a fair concern and we’ll find out next quarter if it is a real issue. Watch adjusted cash opex in Q3 earnings to validate. 

If we take midpoint of Q3 revenue range at $147M and with 50% margin you clear roughly $73M of gross profit. Opex flat near $93M in Q3 means the spend was front-loaded as Eric stated and if that happens then Q4 profitability is certainly in play. If opex isn’t flat, we’ll need correspondingly higher revenue than what was guided. 

I think we will be back above $10 once analyst reports come out. 🤞


*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/bufN/dollaronds-q2-earnings-report-*

### Post #447: 2026-08-14 01:05

**Why is AfterHour in a State of Decline**

Are you sad watching this app die a slow death? Same. Let’s fix it. 

Everyone’s answer as to the reason for the decline is the same: @SirJack checked out, the bugs never got fixed, no new features. Some will even mention the Great AH Coin Rug Pull of ‘25. I don’t buy these reasons as the primary cause. I think they are easy excuses.

Communities tolerate absurd amounts of jank when they’re getting value. Twitter fell over weekly for years and people kept posting. Reddit’s interface was actively hostile for a decade and it became the front page of the internet anyway. Nobody leaves a room they want to be in because the door sticks. When people start citing the product, they’ve usually already left emotionally and are reaching for a reason that doesn’t cost them anything to say out loud.

Here’s what I think is actually going on.

It’s harder to hide a bad call here. On X you post a thesis, it blows up, you scroll past it and post something else. The feed is a firehose and nobody’s keeping score (unless you’re Kevin Xu and then everybody on X is tracking every call you make 😂).
But here it’s a small room and people remember. Your bad call sits in the same feed as everyone who watched you make it. So when shit turns south, it’s hard to own it, to remind everyone that you “fucked up.” Silence gets built into every drawdown, and that’s a feature of a small, accountable community. 

The best posters graduate. You build an audience here, then take it somewhere you can monetize it. Substack, X, wherever. Without a way to keep that value on-platform, this is a farm system, and the top decile is permanently walking out the door. Right, @SIRJACK? 

Thesis exhaustion. We talk a lot about the same handful of tickers through discovery → crowd → catalyst → resolution. After resolution there’s nothing left to say. If new ideas aren’t entering the funnel, the feed stagnates. 

And then reverse Metcalfe does the rest. Network value scales with the square of participants, which means it falls that way too. You post, get one reply instead of four, and you feel like you’re shouting into a canyon. So you post less. But your posts were generating someone else’s replies, so their density drops too, and around it goes. The decline overshoots whatever triggered it by a mile, and then gets blamed on whatever’s most visible. Usually the product.

So fix the loop, not the app.
1. Post your losers. The drawdown posts are worth ten times the victory laps and most people don’t post them. 
2. Comment density is the whole ballgame and it’s the one input we fully control. 
3. Bring a name nobody here is talking about instead of the eleventh take on the same ticker.
4. When someone new posts a real thesis, engage with it like it matters, because that’s the difference between them coming back tomorrow and not.

This app is merely the wrapper, not the product. We are the product. Our content, our replies, our likes. This app doesn’t exist without our engagement. So let’s fix the product we can actually control, ourselves. 

*steps off soap box*


*Link: https://afterhour.com/YungEmber/bug5/why-is-afterhour-in-a-state-of*
